ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u1	Dear fellow Members, fellow Members, ladies and gentlemen! I begin by continuing the 63rd extraordinary meeting of the National Assembly. I am informed that today's meetings cannot be attended by the following Member and Member: Monika Gregorčič from 14:00 to 18:00, Mateja Udovč from 13:00 to 16:00, Jerca Korče from 12 noon, Yuri Lepi from 13:00 to 18:00, and Dr. Matthew T. Vatovec from 12 noon. By the time limit set by the College on the basis of Article 93a(3) of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, no Member or Member has announced for the day of the participation in a meeting outside the meeting of the National Assembly using a remote meeting application. I say hello to everyone who's here! We're moving to 2. At the end of the day, this is a second defensive proposal for a law on the Jamming of the Republic of Slovenia in the Pan-European Guarantee Fund, an urgent procedure. The draft law was tabled by the Government for consideration by the National Assembly. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, State Secretary at the Ministry of Finance mag. Peter Ješovnik.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u2	Mr President, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! In the framework of the covida-19 pandemic, the Group of the European Investment Bank was invited to significantly strengthen its business in support of companies established in the European Union affected by the crisis. Therefore, the Pan-European Guarantee Fund was established as a response. This Fund is part of the overall package of measures agreed upon by the Eurogroup and endorsed by the European Council. The objective of the Pan-European Guarantee Fund is to respond to the economic impact of the covida-19 pandemic by providing companies in participating Member States with sufficient short-term liquidity to cope with the crisis and to continue their growth and development over the medium term. The Fund, with a target of EUR 25 billion in the case of contributions from all Member States, is managed by the EIB Group. By exploiting its own resources and mobilizing additional private sector funding, the EIB aims to generate up to €200 billion in additional funding. Through the Pan-European Guarantee Fund, the EIB Group, in cooperation with selected financial intermediaries, will provide a number of ownership, debt and guarantee products in favour of small and medium-sized enterprises. An invitation to tender for the application of interest has already been published in order to select financial intermediaries from participating Member States in accordance with standard procedures. The Pan-European Guarantee Fund scheme is welcome for the Slovenian economy as it complements the market funding offer and ensures that Slovenian companies will have access to quality and programme-oriented instruments through qualified financial intermediaries. The purpose of this proposal is to provide the legal basis for the Republic of Slovenia to accede to the Pan-European Guarantee Fund in order to guarantee this Fund in a proportionate manner. By this law, the Republic of Slovenia would provide a guarantee of its proportional share in the EIB, which is approximately 0.28 per cent of the point or slightly more than 70 million euro in the period until 31 December 2037. In the event of the adoption of this Act, the Republic of Slovenia would conclude with the EIB a guarantee agreement or an English guarantee agreement and a contribution agreement or an English Contribution Agreement. These are agreements whose content is the same for all participating Member States. Participation by Member States is voluntary and so far 21 Member States have joined the fund. The objective of this Fund is different from the objectives of the measures already taken by the State, which dealt with the direct liquidity problems of Slovenian companies as a priority in order to survive the crisis. In the case of the Pan-European Guarantee Fund, this is a coordinated approach for Member States to move forward, namely the resolution of financial undertakings demonstrating the ability of medium- or long-term development growth. Since the proposed law is the basis for the issue of a guarantee and the conclusion of an agreement with the EIB, in order to enable the participation of Slovenian financial intermediaries and thereby the access of Slovenian companies to the products of the Pan-European Guarantee Fund, I propose that the law be supported. Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u3	Thank you very much. The draft law was considered by the Committee on Finance as the working body of the mother. For the presentation of the Committee's report, I give the floor to President Robert Polnar.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u4	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! At its 32nd meeting, on 24 February 2021, the Finance Committee considered the Proposal for a Law on the Guarantee of the Republic of Slovenia in the Pan-European Guarantee Fund submitted by the Government to the National Assembly for its consideration and adoption following an urgent procedure. In accordance with Rule 129 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, amendments to the draft law have been tabled by the Coalition's parliamentary groups, namely the three articles. In an additional introductory explanation, the State Secretary from the Ministry of Finance presented the objective and purpose of the proposal for a law whereby Slovenia, as a co-founder, can join the Pan-European Guarantee Fund and provide a guarantee in proportion to the share of the fund. Through the fund, companies will indirectly access liquid assets to overcome the consequences of the pandemic. The representative of the Legal and Legislative Service said that the amendments had been tabled on the basis of the opinion of the Service and that most of the comments made by the Service with these amendments had been taken into account. In the debate, the members and members of the committee supported the proposal of the law by ensuring that liquid assets can be obtained for businesses. Some have been severely affected by the crisis and will need additional funds to finance operating assets and investments. During the discussion, the members of the Committee were interested in the question of the possibility of applying for an invitation to tender or whether the SID of the Bank could do so as well as in the assessment of the guarantees given in terms of their possible redemption. The fact that the deposits of companies in Slovenian business banks increased significantly in 2020 was also highlighted. During and after the discussion, the Secretary of State replied to the questions raised and provided further clarifications. In accordance with the second paragraph of Article 130 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee considered and adopted the amendments tabled by the members of the coalition. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee then voted on and adopted all the articles of the bill. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the amended Act, which includes the amendments adopted, is prepared on the basis of Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly and is an integral part of this report. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u5	Thank you very much. The following is a presentation of the views of the groups of Members. Miha Kordiš will present the position of the Left Group.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u6	In Levica, we do not oppose European liquidity solutions to public institutions and businesses within the European Union, in particular with a view to maintaining employment and covering employee income losses, but we do not support the Pan-European Guarantee Fund in its current design. The fund will not provide liquidity directly to companies, but will provide guarantees to banks to issue loans to businesses. So it's basically again about bank resolution and not business. Such a solution may benefit countries from the centre with a strong domestic banking sector, such as Germany. For Slovenia, where you, ladies and gentlemen have privatised most of the banks, it means, above all, protecting foreign banking capital against potential losses. But the banking sector remains practically the only sector that has not been significantly affected by the epidemic, including by us. The profit of the pre-tax banking system was €472 million last year. In the last financial stability report, the Bank of Slovenia stressed that the resilience of the Slovenian banking system remains good even during a period of tight economic conditions. Most of the funds spent by the State to help businesses and the population ended up in private banks in foreign ownership. Last year, when the country produced 3.5 billion euros in deficits, the balance sheet sum of Slovenian banks increased by 3.4 billion euros. One would think that when we have such well-capitalised banks, we can expect to be able to lend generously to the population and the economy, but the opposite has happened. Banks of this inflow of money, which comes largely from the state minority, are filled by all citizens and citizens, have not spent on spending credit to the domestic population and businesses. Why? Because it's not profitable for them. Credits to the domestic non-bank sector fell by 1.4% in 2020; at the same time – watch out – credit growth for foreigners with Slovenian money increased by 28%. Slovenian banks play roughly the same role as private health institutions in solving the current crisis. The provider of crisis resolution is public health, and health care professionals are not paid to deal with the crisis, so they are nowhere to be seen except when they demand the assistance of the state. Private banks do not increase the lending of the local population, nor do they finance key investments because, as I have already mentioned, this is not worth it. On the contrary, with the help of countries, they contribute to the flight of capital abroad and then become loud when they need a country to provide credit guarantees and thus protect them against inherited investments through public money. It's like we've never seen this script in this country before. In the event of problems, banks will simply collect state guarantees and no longer deal with businesses, and countries will be stabbed by the rehabilitation of their next business. This is how countries share risks and private banks privatise profits; despite the fact that banks have strong support from the ECB and only the budgetary resources they need or need to strengthen the social state, public health and the public pension system. The current situation has not come from somewhere, but from past harmful political decisions. After the last crisis in the banks, the government's political set-up swept away €4.5 billion of public money and then sold it for change to foreigners. Now, at the peak of the epidemic, these privatised banks have realised almost half a billion euros in profits, of which we do not have the slightest benefit, and people in this country suffer and are barely breaking through from day to day. The solution for Slovenia is therefore not State guarantees for foreign private banks, the solution is diametrically the opposite – strengthening the SID of the bank as the only serious financial institution left in the hands of the State and renationalising the Slovenian system banks. Only in this way will we force banks that do not currently play the basic role of lending to the domestic economy to start investing in projects that will serve the country's development, social and environmental objectives; not to fill the pockets of foreign owners. Instead of the Pan-European Guarantee Fund, which protects the profits of bank capital, the only serious alternative is the nationalisation of systemic banks. After all, a country that does not control its banking system cannot consider itself a sovereign state, but rather a colony of the financial market.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u7	Thank you very much. Jožef Horvat will present the position of the Nova Slovenija Group – Christian Democrats.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u8	Thank you for the floor, Mr President. Good afternoon, colleagues and colleagues! Nice greeting to the Secretary of State, Mag. Yeshovnik! On 19 May last year, the informal ECOFIN meeting, which, of course, also includes our Slovenian Finance Minister, Mr Mag. Andrej Shircelj, met to take note of the progress made in implementing the agreed measures to mitigate the effects of the covida-19 epidemic. At that time, on 19 May last year, for information only, a regulation on the launch of the SURE instrument was also approved, with which the European Union wants to protect – and in fact it already works – jobs and workers during the current crisis. At that time, last May, the finance ministers also set out the basic dimensions of the third pillar of the bailout package, represented by the Pan-European Guarantee Fund, which would enable the European Investment Bank to increase its lending activity by up to EUR 200 billion, with a focus on small and medium-sized enterprises. Today we have on the table the Proposal for a Law on Guarantee of the Republic of Slovenia in the Pan-European Guarantee Fund. The purpose of this law is to ensure that Slovenia, as co-founder of the Pan-European Guarantee Fund and in a proportionate proportion, guarantees this fund. In this way, the Slovenian economy is given access to additional liquidity assets, which are a particular problem at this time, to overcome the crisis as the consequences of covida-19. The programme loans guaranteed by the Pan-European Guarantee Fund will be intended to finance operating assets and investments. The Fund will allow the European Investment Bank Group, with a total guarantee of EUR 25 billion, to increase its financial support to European companies, including Slovenian companies, to an additional EUR 200 billion; with emphasis on – which is particularly important for New Slovenia – small and medium-sized enterprises. The State guarantee shall cover in proportion the risk of default of all loans secured by the funds of this fund, irrespective of the country from which the recipient does not repay the loans. The Pan-European Guarantee Fund provides for the contribution of all 27 Member States of the European Union in the form of guarantees and is also available for contributions from other entities. The expected net loss of the fund is also estimated at 20%, which means that the amount of the call for guarantees is estimated to reach that proportion, i.e. 20%. The guarantee provides for the coverage of loans to small and medium-sized enterprises, also limited to large companies and legal persons of public law who carry out activities in the field of health care, health research or the provision of services related to the health crisis; this is also extremely important for us in New Slovenia. The financing of end-users will be carried out through financial intermediaries and banks which will obtain the guarantee of this fund for this purpose. The guarantee of this fund covers up to 75 % of the loan to the final recipients, the remaining risk is borne by financial intermediaries and banks that have obtained the guarantee. We are pleased that, in the second reading of the Committee on Finance, we have prepared the appropriate text of the Proposal for a Law on the Guarantee of the Republic of Slovenia in the Pan-European Guarantee Fund, which will also be supported by the New Slovenia Group at the vote in the National Assembly. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u10	Dear Secretary of State, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! With the adoption of the law we are considering today, Slovenia will join the Pan-European Guarantee Fund, which is under the auspices of the European Union. The aim of the Fund is to increase the liquidity of the economy, which has been crippled by the epidemic crisis in the past year. As a result, companies do not achieve the credit ratings required by private lenders. That is why Europe will mobilise additional funding from the private sector through Member States' guarantees. The fund will guarantee loans totalling EUR 200 billion. In order for the Fund to actually come to life, it must be supported by a sufficient number of members, who will guarantee guarantees in proportion to their share. The share guaranteed by Slovenia amounts to slightly less than 0,3 % of the Fund's total assets, but the risk of redemption is actually estimated at EUR 14 million. Guarantees for loans are specifically intended for small and medium-sized enterprises. Large companies and legal persons of public law will also benefit from these activities, which carry out activities related to the health crisis. In the first wave of the epidemic, Slovenia adopted a domestic guarantee scheme. We have not opposed it in the SAB, but we have warned from the outset that the conditions are inadequate and that loans with State guarantee will only be leased by large companies with high revenues from sales in 2019. Only the sixth anti-korona package provided a guarantee of 25% of the revenue, which raised the demand slightly, but unfortunately for many companies it was too late and still inadequate. In the SAB we support the guarantee scheme, but we call on both the government and the competent ministry to inform the public and stakeholders of the domestic economy in a timely and appropriate manner of the conditions and criteria that companies will have to meet to obtain guarantees. We do not want new episodes that we have witnessed in the procurement of medical and protective equipment and rapid tests in which certain suppliers are linked to the government. We do not want new episodes that we have witnessed in the event of the government's inappropriate communication with the innkeepers who only learned in the second wave of the epidemic that they could carry out their activities, as did those who were close to the government. In the SAB, we want to provide guarantees to domestic companies for loans under the same conditions. To avoid the above-mentioned episodes, we appeal to the government and to the Ministry of the Interior for clear, timely and correct information on the basis of which accession to the European Guarantee Scheme will enable all and not only selected companies. In the SAB Group, we support the bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u12	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen of Parliament! The European Union is trying to organise a successful fiscal defence for the period of post-pandemic shock, and it must, in order to halt the upcoming crisis, go to the end of the historically unrecorded fiscal expansion measures. The European Investment Bank wants to create up to €200 billion of additional funding for the economy, in particular small and medium-sized enterprises, their liquidity and operating assets. The State guarantee is intended to cover, in proportion to the proportion, the risk of non-refunding of all loans secured under this fund, irrespective of the country from which the recipient does not repay the loans. In fact, it is less important whether the projected volume of the guarantee fund will amount to EUR 200 billion or more or less. The fundamental question that is being raised is whether this is how a fiscal union within the European Union is slowly created. In the first round, this could only be a response to the epidemic and later a certain level of fiscal community could likely become a characteristic of the Union. The fiscal union would mean that the central government – the European Commission – could devote money to the less developed and the European Parliament could adopt laws on various tax obligations, which would interfere with the sovereignty of the Member States. Wealthier would be given, although some of them are convinced that this is not worth it, but the fiscal account is different from the economic one, so the most powerful countries are interested in organising at least a limited fiscal union. From their point of view, it is not irrelevant how many products and services can be sold to less developed members; however, in order to do so, the people of these countries must have sufficient purchasing power. Investment in these countries therefore indirectly leads to an increase in their economic growth. The crisis caused by the virus has halted economic growth in a completely new way, with which we have not had experience so far. The Pan-European Guarantee Fund has become operational in September 2020, it seems that its creators understand the nature of the current crisis and are now in the process of establishing agreed and coordinated policies. Almost every business decision in the company has to deal with the company's finances sooner or later. We must be aware that the essence of the company's funding is to know the financial implications of preparing, adopting, implementing, controlling and analysing decisions on the financing of the company. It is therefore a question of whether the company has the money and whether the available amount of money is in line with the size of the Western liabilities and more – with the size of the desired development of the company. A broader definition means that in addition to the current capacity to pay, the company also has a long-term capacity to pay and thus money to finance development. However, an important finding regarding liquidity is that the availability of money always requires costs, whether real or opportunities. The optimum payment capacity means achieving liquidity as available cash in the amount of outstanding liabilities, taking into account the principles of the cost-effectiveness of the supply of funding resources, the correct prices of those resources and the profitable use of funding resources expressed through the return on the equity unit. The financial consequences of business decisions must be known in the company by all those involved in decision-making. Members of the Desus Group therefore point out that, from the point of view of its existence, no State intervention or any such or other guarantee can replace the financial strategies and tactics of each company. We will support the proposal for a law on the guarantee of the Republic of Slovenia in the Pan-European Guarantee Fund. Thank you very much.

A Committee of Investors will be established to oversee the activities of this Fund. Each Member State will have its own member and alternate member of the committee to decide with the partner committee on the fair allocations of the Fund's funds. We will send our representatives there, but they will, as the standard, contribute to large-scale decisions and invest in different European companies. And if the big ones decide that by excluding Slovenia they will also receive companies, foreign companies, and when there is a guarantee for these investments, they will certainly not exclude us. The bill is harmful and dangerous for Slovenia, and there is no need for foreigners under the influence of large international capital to decide which Slovenian company deserves or does not deserve. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u21	Thank you very much. Monika Gregorčič will present the position of the Group of the Party of the Modern Centre.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u22	Mr President, Secretary of State, Members and Members of Parliament! We support the proposal for a law on the guarantee of the Republic of Slovenia in the Pan-European Guarantee Fund in the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre. We estimate that this is one of the good measures that will help the economy recover faster after the epidemic at European Member State level. The European Commission describes the interlocking of all the countercyclical measures taken as three safety nets. The SURE instrument is a safety net for workers, the European Investment Bank fund is a safety net for businesses, and the European Stability Mechanism loans are security nets for countries. This whole fiscal package totals €540 billion. The aid measure increases the role of the European Investment Bank through the European Investment Fund guarantee schemes and through the establishment of the Pan-European Guarantee Fund of EUR 25 billion. By mobilising additional private sector funding, however, there is a desire to generate up to €200 billion of aid to the economy. The Fund provides guarantees to national and local banks and other financial intermediaries in order to enable financing to small and medium-sized enterprises which would be more difficult to obtain loans as a result of the crisis. Companies may so request funding directly or from financial institutions in their countries. The participating countries shall, however, provide guarantees proportionate to their share in the EIB. State guarantees will thus cover potential losses that may arise in the EIB's operations. The deadline for applications and approvals of projects is limited to 31 March 2021, with an extension of 6 months; however, it is envisaged that the members of the Fund will provide guarantees by 31 December 2037. In the SMC Group, we agree that although Slovenia is relatively good at the liquidity situation of our economy for the time being, due to the possible continuation of the crisis, it must also allow companies access to new guarantee schemes. That is why our country joins this Pan-European Guarantee Fund, although it is not among the first. While the Fund has been operational since October 2020, it has been joined by Member States which together form more than 90% of the capital of the European Investment Bank. It was not until December that the European Commission approved the establishment of the Fund or found that its operation was in accordance with State aid rules. However, it is difficult to predict whether and to what extent our companies and financial intermediaries will serve this measure at all. Also because in Slovenia we are in a different position from other members of the European Union because of large fiscal incentives for both the population and businesses and because of the complementary national guarantee schemes under anti-Corona legislation. That our economy is in relatively good condition is also seen in Banka Slovenije reports. These reports show that the financing of banks with bank loans is shrinking and banks are increasing the role of companies that have a liquidity reserve nature during the crisis and can later be the source of new investments. Therefore, in the SMC Group, we are also thinking in order for businesses to benefit from an extension of the national guarantee measure from the so-called seventh CAP, which expires in November this year. As already mentioned, we support today's proposal for the bill in the Group of the Parties to the Modern Centre. Thank you.

Today, on the proposal of the three ministries, we are adopting a proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on State Administration with a clear objective of effective implementation of the tasks of individual ministries, which we welcome and support in Novi Slovenija. Slovenia is the third most forested country in the European Union for Finland and Sweden, with an annual growth of almost 9 million cubic metres of wood. Although the wood industry is on the rise, we do not yet make sufficient use of this natural wealth. In Novi Slovenija we advocate a shift of the forest economy to the processing of low-carbon materials that enable energy self-sustainment and environmentally friendly green industry. In Slovenia, we are not aware of the exceptional importance of the wood industry in providing greater self-sustainment and self-sufficiency. Christian Democrats in the future expect active assistance from the country's forestry industry also by removing bureaucratic barriers to obtaining various permits, where the time component seriously hampers the development of forestry in Slovenia. The second amendment governs the transfer of the powers of military graves from the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and equal opportunities to the Ministry of Defence, where the Military Heritage Authority will be established to this end, which will act in the composition of the Ministry. In 2003 the War Cemeteries Act was passed in the Republic of Slovenia and in 2015 the Law on Hidden War Cemeteries and the Burial of Victims was adopted. The fact is that the Republic of Slovenia has long not developed an institution that deals with hidden war graves. Under the rule of Borut Pahor in 2010, the War Cemetery Service was even disbanded. And right on this day, it will be twelve years now since the discovery of the tomb in the grave pit. Modern trends in the development of military history at home and abroad recommend linking museum, research and education activities, as this is the only way to preserve military cultural heritage. In the light of the invisible enemy and thus the new facts at the time of entry into the country and the poor management of property in the border crossing area, we welcome the last addition that the Ministry of Interior becomes the manager of these properties during the period until 1 January 2022. ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u26	Dear President of the National Assembly, fellow Member and fellow Members, invited guests! The State Administration is part of the public administration and could be designated as an instrument of the State for the performance of its function. At the central level, this function is primarily carried out by the government and the ministries. Article 14 of the Law on State Administration provides for ministries to be set up to perform administrative tasks in one or more administrative areas. Accordingly, the work areas of individual ministries are also set out. The proposal for a law introduces changes in the working areas of certain ministries. It is the Ministry of Defence, the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities and the Ministry of Economic Development and Technology. The proposal states that the proposed amendments will have a positive impact on the functioning and performance of the tasks of the ministries mentioned above. Thus, the Ministry of Defence is involved in the field of war disabled persons, war veterans and victims of war violence and war graves. These areas now fall within the competence of the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities. At first, the bill provided for the transfer of powers to the Ministry of Defence exclusively in the field of military graves. In addition, later additions added the area of war invalids, war veterans, and victims of war violence. This raises the question why the Ministry of Labour, Society and Social Affairs transfer the field of disabled persons to the Ministry of Labour. It is true that this status is due to the exercise of military duties, but in the SAB we are concerned about what this will mean for the rights of disabled persons in the context of disabled persons, social protection and employment policies. The proposal also adds to the work area of the Ministry of Economic Development and Technology. In the law, forestry has not been particularly exposed to the Ministry of Economic Development and Technology, although the Ministry has already done a number of tasks in this area. Among other things, on the basis of an action plan to increase the competitiveness of the forest and forest chain in Slovenia under the MGRT, the Forestry Directorate was established in 2015. This has placed the timber industry among strategically important and promising industries. An additional addition to the bill proposed at the meeting of the Committee provides for the transfer of the area of property management in the border crossing area to the management of the Ministry of the Interior. If this means that this infrastructure, which is largely neglected, is regulated and modernised, we welcome such a solution to the SAB. In the SAB, we agree that it is right for certain related tasks or powers to be combined in one place, which, of course, will affect the more transparent functioning of the whole government, but it is important to be careful, especially when the areas of treatment of the most vulnerable groups and their rights are transferred. In the SAB Group, we will not oppose the bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u28	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen of Parliament! The public administration is, in a functional or material sense, an area of state activities dealing with the direct enforcement of laws and other regulations. This is done through the adoption of individual administrative acts and through the provision of professional services and material and technical tasks; in a formal or organisational sense, it is a system of public and public institutions exercising public powers. The effectiveness of the operation of individual subsystems depends on how they are managed by the holders of the highest powers, rationalisation and greater prudence cannot be created with rapid cuts, but gradually between individual sectors. The proposed amendment to the Law on State Administration follows the objectives thus set. The amendments to the organisational structure and working areas of the three ministries, namely, the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities, the Ministry of Defence and the Ministry of Economic Development and Technology, are proposed. The area of treatment of military graves will be transferred through organizational change from the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and equal opportunities to the Ministry of Defence. Work areas will also be transferred to war disabled persons, war veterans and victims of war violence. The Ministry of Defence is where they are already regulating their status and rights. Simplification and envisaged efficiency of work will thus be achieved through a meaningful link between related areas within one ministry. The Ministry of Economic Development and Technology formalizes the establishment of a structure through legal change, the constitutional part of which is also the Directorate for Forestry. It implements its incentive and co-financing policies as part of its decision on the timber industry as a strategically important and promising economic sector. Slovenia is one of the most forested countries in Europe. According to the Institute for Forests, the forest area is somewhat less than a million and 200 thousand hectares or more than half of the country’s surface area. In other words, the forestry of Slovenia is 58% and the annual growth of wood is estimated at 8 million and 800 thousand cubic metres. Nor can it be overlooked that 21% of the forests are owned by the state, 3% are owned by municipalities and 76% are privately owned. Larger and narrower forest holdings of state forests make it possible to manage economically and more easily. But the problem is a fragmented private forest estate. According to the latest data, there are already 286,000 forest properties in Slovenia, owned by as many as 413 thousand owners or co-owners. Such extensive fragmentation makes professional work and optimal use of wood in private forests difficult. Members of the Desus Group support the Directorate for Forestry's efforts in the systematic resolution of this important economic sector. Growth in this area needs to be accelerated through development policies as well as fiscal measures. We need to make sure that growth is qualitative. However, the quality of growth based on the use of domestic raw materials, productive investments in the ability to produce products of higher value added and on a solid structure of quality exports is qualitative. The draft amendment to the Law on State Administration will be supported by Members of the Desus Group. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u42	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Fellow Members and fellow Members, and a respected government wherever you are! The fundamental objective of the new Law on ID card is to comply with the European acquis. The European Regulation on the safety of identity cards requires Member States to harmonise legislation on biometric identity cards by 2 August 2021. This is therefore the last time Slovenia will have to start issuing biometric ID cards. The security levels of national identity cards issued by Member States vary considerably. Such differences increase the risk of falsification and misuse of documents. This has also led the European Union to harmonise the rules on identity cards in all Member States. The new ID card will contain a chip that will store a record of two fingerprints and a photo of the identity card holder. The use of biometric data on the storage medium will be limited solely to verifying the authenticity of the identity of the holder in crossing the national border. Such identity cards, including fingerprints, will be issued to citizens after the age of 12. In Slovenia, biometric passports have been issued since 2006, so the novels follow the solutions already laid down in the Travel Charter Act. The Regulation provides that biometric data should be stored safely and only until the document is taken over and in any event no more than 90 days after the date of the document’s acquisition, then biometric data must be deleted or destroyed immediately. At the same time, the overall use of the ID card will increase for legal representation, as data on the country and place of birth and postal and postal numbers will be added. From now on, the identity card can also be used as an electronic identification tool. It will enable the application to e-government services, electronic signature and use of the ID card as a substitute for a health insurance card if users so wish. In doing so, we are proposing in the SAB that, following the example of neighbouring countries, such as Austria, the possibility that the health insurance card is also a European health insurance card. During the legislative process, the prevalence of the use of the ID card has created some concerns about a certain age, particularly with regard to the protection of the child’s benefit. The Legislative and Legal Service has warned that there is a possibility of certain risks if the electronic identity linked to the ID card is allocated to children after the age of 12 years. We also agree in the SAB that it would be more appropriate to set the age at 15 years of age, at least for electronic identification. Only persons who have completed at least 15 years of age can gain their business ability. The bill also clearly sets out cases in which the consent of both parents is not necessary when applying for a child’s identity card. ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u48	Thank you, President for your word. Very nice greetings all present, colleagues and colleagues! Today, we have a summary of the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Law on ID card. The reason for this is that on 2 August 2021 the Republic of Slovenia, in accordance with Regulation (EU) 2019/1157 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 20 June 2019 on strengthening the safety of Union citizens' identity cards and residence documents issued to Union citizens and their family members exercising their right to freedom of movement, will have to start issuing biometric identity cards. Thus we will have a biometric identity card in addition to the biometric passport, which we welcome very much in the Slovenian Democratic Party. The Regulation of the European Parliament follows the objectives of the Treaty on European Union, which is to facilitate the free movement of persons, which means the right to enter and leave the Member States by means of a valid identity card or passport. This underlines the importance of mandatory systematic verification of all persons and their travel documents in the Schengen Information System and of Interpol's collection of data on stolen and lost documents irrespective of the nationality of the holder. It is important to note that more than one billion people travel each year within the European Union and cross its external borders. At the same time, the proposal is based on ensuring the safety of travel documents, identifying documents as one of the key elements in the fight against terrorism and organised crime and, as such, is part of an action plan to strengthen the European response to travel documents fraud. Personal ID cards shall be issued in accordance with national law to 26 EU Member States and the holding of identity cards shall be compulsory in 15 Member States. It was found that the safety levels of national identity cards issued by Member States vary greatly. These differences increase the risk of falsification and abuse of these documents and also give citizens practical problems when they want to exercise the right to freedom of movement. However, the number of frauds with ID cards is increasing. The establishment of minimum safety standards and the inclusion of biometric data in identity cards are therefore important steps towards improving the safety of the use of identity cards in the Union. The introduction of minimum protection elements and taking into account the uniform form of identity cards will allow Member States to rely on the credibility of the documents issued when Union citizens exercise their right to freedom of movement. The EU Regulation also takes into account the aspect of the protection of personal data to be processed in the context of the application of this Regulation, stressing the importance of protecting the measures applicable to sensitive data such as biometric identifiers. With regard to biometric data, the amendment provides that two fingerprints will be taken in a prescribed manner in addition to a photo of citizens from the age of 12. Exemptions for withdrawal may only be health reasons. The fingerprints in the record of issued identity cards shall be kept for as long as the identity card is served, but not more than 90 days from the date of issue of the identity card. Where the fingerprinting is temporarily not possible, the identity card shall be issued with one-year validity. The identity card shall include, inter alia, a chip containing biometric data of an individual who can only be used to verify the authenticity of the identity of the identity of the holder. The proposal to amend the law also provides for a correction in practice by detecting shortcomings. This also adds mail and postal number to the ID card. For those who do not actually have a place in Slovenia or a temporary or permanent residence, they will have “without residence” in their identity card. However, since the European Parliament Regulation allows a personal card issued after 12 years of age to contain means for electronic identification, the chip on the ID card may also contain a qualified certificate for electronic signature, thus facilitating access to electronic services by amending the law governing the field of electronic identification. The bill has slightly higher financial implications, which is linked to the construction of an information structure that will allow the issuance of new biometric cards. It is also necessary to ensure the acquisition of additional information equipment for fingerprinting when data capture is not possible in the administrative unit. This is mainly the case when administrative staff, for example, will have to take fingerprints at the home of older citizens or at home to an individual, but the cost is negligible compared to what the identity card brings to citizens and citizens in the future. In addition, the use of a new ID card as a substitute for a health insurance card is envisaged in the future, which is already in line with the Health Insurance Office. As we can see, the proposal to amend the law has a great impact on the privacy of people or on their personal data and has therefore been aligned with the information officer. To conclude with certainty, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia is following digital developments and is looking at the long-term implementation of new solutions, which means that the proposed amendments to the Law on Identity Card are aimed at taking on more modernisation by one move. This will not cause citizens and citizens to have to change their ID card more than once, nor will they be subject to additional costs. In addition, the government of Janez Janša points to operationalisation, since Regulation (EU) 2019/1157 was adopted already in the time of the Sharčev government and has not been implemented in the Slovenian legal order. This government will bring this amendment of the law into line with the legal order of the European Union. In the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian Democratic Party, we will unanimously support the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Personal Card Act. Thank you.

At this point, there are 5,000 758 residence procedures. The essence of the law is to prevent a fictitious application, so we have ensured by the legal provisions that it will no longer be possible to apply anywhere in administrative units, but only on a local unit. Locally competent because administrative units in the past have found that someone who wanted to register a temporary or permanent residence in the area such as Slovenske Bistrica was not satisfied there or did not do so simply went to the administrative unit in Koper, where he was temporarily or permanently registered in Slovenska Bistrica. This remains, but only to those where you are locally competent. In order not to do so again, we also introduce a so-called verification of procedures by official duty, where an official in the administrative unit has to verify certain data. We're introducing a short-term administrative verification procedure. If the administrative unit in force today initiates a verification procedure, such verification shall take at least a year or up to two years before the witness is invited before the mail is received and so on. Here, however, I can argue that we are in some way introducing the opposite burden of proof, where the party must prove that it has the conditions for a person to report; or a person to prove that he is entitled to apply at a given address. Surface standards are urgently needed, so with the enforcement of this law, an administrative officer will have the opportunity and will have to check surface standards through Gurs. Gurs in some ways ensures that more than 80% of surface standards are entered into electronic computer devices. ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u62	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Ladies and gentlemen of Parliament! The institution of residence registration is being abused a lot. Not surprisingly, there are a lot of rights at the address of residence, so in practice it happens that individuals apply for residence on addresses where they do not actually reside. On this basis, they are entitled primarily to different forms of social transfers, which they would not otherwise belong to. Amendments and additions to the law address the problem of abuse that occurs in the area of applications and residence cancellations, either permanent or temporary. It is the fictitious residence of individuals on addresses not intended for residential use and on addresses where the size of the useful surface does not clearly allow for the actual residence of more people. We are also reminded of the need to regulate these matters by events that took place in the area of administrative units Maribor and Krško. More than 100 people were also reported at one address. Infringements, and even in the commission of criminal offences, have also been freely facilitated by officials. The number of actual residence procedures is increasing from year to year. However, this is nothing more than statistical information, for control is ineffective and, above all, still insufficient. According to the new report, an official in the administrative unit will be required to verify certain information relating to the system of the Geodetic Administration of the Republic of Slovenia. All those who do not yet have regulated data or spatial property records and will wish to register residence on such property will have to do so within a certain period of time. In the Desus Group, we view as the most important reintroduction of the local jurisdiction of the application of residence and the provision that registration will be possible only in residential properties. It is expected that employees in administrative units know their areas better than they do elsewhere in Slovenia, so with much greater reliability they can find that the facility in which they wish to apply is not a residential property; and thus prevent the intended abuse. The bill also extends a set of evidence or legal grounds on the right to reside on the basis of an applicant’s address. As evidence that an individual has the right to reside on the title, the declaration of ownership or co-ownership, rental or rental contract, the consent of the owner, one co-owner or landlord shall at present be taken into account. In addition, as evidence of the right to reside, the contract on the service of the apartment, the contract of subsistence and the proof of building rights are also defined as proof of the right to reside. The official person of the administrative unit shall be required in the official records to verify the ownership or co-ownership data, unless the applicant submits it himself. The novelty also lays down a new condition for applying for permanent residence relating to surface standards. The purpose of the proposal is to prevent fictitious entries at the addresses of buildings that do not actually allow residence. We need slightly clearer criteria as to how this area is determined, and more importantly, how to determine in the process that a given area is not sufficient. The Regulation establishing minimum standards for the accommodation of foreigners who are employed or working in the Republic of Slovenia, which was adopted under the Employment and Labour Act, is applicable. Article 2 of this Regulation specifies the minimum surface area according to the number of persons staying in a specified facility, as well as other minimum living and hygiene standards. We believe that the meaningful application of this or the adoption of a similar rule would solve a number of dilemmas. Members of the Desus Group are aware that the institution of residence registration is too often misused and that the regulation of this area is essential, so we will support the proposal for a new law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u77	Thank you very much. Mag. Andrej Reich will present the position of the Party's Parliamentary Group, Alenka Bratušek.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u78	Honourable Vice-President, Honourable Secretary of State, Fellow Member and Members. The proposal for amendments and the addition of the Residence Registration Act was primarily created in response to the issue of fictitious residence registrations. In the course of the inspections, it was found that more than 100 people were reported at certain addresses, but that they did not actually live there. Moreover, the fictitious application of foreigners to temporary residence for some is also a profitable but illegal business. The abuse of social transfers is also closely linked to this problem. The Interdepartmental Working Group has also drawn up baselines for amendments to the Residence Registration Act. The working group was set up to examine the issue of fictitious residence applications. One of the changes is the reintroduction of local jurisdiction to register residence. As a result, the application of residence will not be possible anywhere in Slovenia, as has been the case so far, but the procedure will have to be carried out on the competent administrative unit. Officials in the administrative unit are familiar with the area of their administrative unit, so the possibility of fictitious reporting by a larger number of individuals will be reduced. Now, in practice, if an individual has failed on one administrative unit, he has simply tried luck on the other. The Novela also introduces surface standards for the number of persons reported to the size of the usable area of the apartment. This will check what the actual usable surface of the building is like when applying to administrative units. These data will be transferred to the Register of Permanent Populations from the records of the Geodetic Authority of the Republic of Slovenia. Surface standards will be established by means of a sub-legislative act, and the SAB reminds us that it will be necessary to be careful when determining them, as some actually live on small areas. The majority of these are socially disadvantaged persons, so it will be necessary to ensure that their situation is not further threatened. Procedures for verifying the authenticity of the application shall also be reduced. Thus, administrative units can be much more effective in preventing and detecting abuses. The novelty also contains some other solutions that address the shortcomings detected in practice. Some refer to applications for children’s residences and some to applications for foreign residences. The law also harmonizes with the Family Code. In the SAB Group, we consider the proposed solutions to be good, but we want them to also bring effective results and prevent abuses in practice. In the past, some activities to prevent fictitious applications have already been carried out. Among other things, an information solution was installed in the Register of Permanent Populations in 2013 to review the number of persons or permanent and temporary residence applications at a specific address. Obviously, however, such a system still does not work in practice and further legal changes are needed. Supplements to the law that we disagree with in the SAB were adopted at the meeting of the parent committee. Thus, at the time of the application of a permanent residence and at the time of the notification or modification of a permanent address abroad, the individual should, inter alia, indicate nationality, religion and mother tongue. That the collection of these data was controversial, the information officer also warned. In doing so, it stated that the proposed amendments did not give rise to what objectives were to be pursued by the collection of these data, nor was the need for collection justified. The Slovenian Constitution also stipulates that no one is obliged to define his or her religious or other beliefs. The amendments tabled to this provision are welcomed in the SAB. If these supplements are eliminated today, we will support the proposal of the SAB Group. Thank you very much.

An important milestone for growth in the number of patients was reached on 4 November 2020 when the number of visible patients in Slovenian hospitals first exceeded the number of thousands and persisted until the beginning of February of this year. The covid-19 disease epidemic has only further burdened health workers and homes for older citizens who were overloaded before the outbreak of the virus. The human resources deficit is increasing in health care and nursing homes, and employees are overburdened, exhausted, and burned out. In addition, health workers have been confronted with increasing verbal and physical violence by patients or their relatives throughout the epidemic, which also endangers their physical and mental health. On the basis of the fifth package of intervention measures, health and social security workers are entitled to an allowance of 30% of the hourly basic wage for direct work with covid-19 patients or users of services to which the infection has been confirmed or suspected. The proposed amendment to the fifth anti-Koronic Law of the PKP5 considers that employees in health and social institutions deserve to be shown more respect for their work and the profession they carry out during these difficult times. Therefore, we propose to increase the staff allowance for direct work with covid-19 patients to 65%, limiting the doubling of this and the crisis allowance under the collective contract for the public sector in the same way as was regulated in the first wave of the epidemic under the CAP3. For employees we also propose two awards in the form of an extraordinary premium payment for the collective additional pension insurance and one additional tourist voucher. Dear colleagues and colleagues, we are even more confident that our proposal justifies your support, because the housing organizations at the peak of the epidemic at the beginning of October 2020 have addressed the competent ministries and the Government of the Republic of Slovenia with the title – Call for Help: we can no longer do so. It says that for more than half a year, selfless and extremely self - sacrificing, sometimes at the edge of power, they save lives and care for the most endangered. Despite their protection, employees are at high risk of infection, and working in full protective equipment is extremely difficult and exhausting. It is therefore time, my fellow Members and colleagues, to show more respect for the employees who are fighting the virus in the first battles by paying the appropriate payment for the work done and the special reward. I thank you in advance for your support. Thank you.

An additional 65% of the hourly wage for working hours in hazardous conditions has also been provided for persons performing work through an external contractor and persons involved in public works. If we go back to the novel under consideration today. The new law provides for, inter alia, a higher allowance for direct work with covid-19 patients who would, in accordance with the proposal, be 65% of the hourly basic wage item, or a supplement adopted at a meeting of the parent committee, not more than 100 % of the hourly basic wage of the employee, including the addition referred to in Article 11(39) of the Collective Contract for the public sector. Here we need to draw attention to what the government has also pointed out that employees working in a red and gray zone are already receiving, for hours, a hazard allowance and a special burden in accordance with the collective agreement, 65% of the hourly basic wage item and 30% of the hourly basic wage item in accordance with the intervention law. Therefore, a total of 95 % of the hourly heading of the basic salary. However, if they are temporarily assigned to work with another employer, they shall receive an additional 30 % of the hourly basic wage item. However, in the case of the payment of the one-time extraordinary premium of the collective additional pension insurance for public servants, it is recalled that all public servants are subject to a single minimum premium of additional pension insurance, plus an additional premium in relation to the completed years of work. The fixing of a one-time extraordinary premium for certain public servants, as proposed by the applicant, would constitute a derogation from the applicable collective supplementary pension scheme, which, according to our assessment, exposes the inequality between these public servants and all other public servants, since all employees of the employer must be included in the collective supplementary pension scheme under the same conditions. We wonder, however, how private health and social security providers may make a fair or comparable payment of the premium to individual supplementary pension insurance if the employees are not included in the collective supplementary pension insurance. PPIZ-2 provides for different entitlements for funds paid into collective supplementary pension insurance and for funds paid into individual supplementary pension insurance, within which a single increase of the savings is possible. This would also put these employees in an unequal position. With regard to an additional voucher in the area of tourism consumption, the aim of such awarding to a narrower group of beneficiaries is raised here, since such a mechanism has been put in place exclusively as an aid to tourism, with all citizens being treated equally; and it has therefore been awarded a voucher in the same amount specifically for adults and especially for children. Therefore, we will not support the Group of the Parties to the modern centre of the bill.

Unlike the proposal under consideration, which concerns only a minor or partial amendment, the government proposal fully addresses the position of the Slovenian Press Agency. The amendments and additions proposed by the Government of the Republic of Slovenia relate to all key areas in the law, from the definition of the activities of the Slovenian Press Agency, the financing of its public service, the principles of operation, the arrangements of the Supervisory Board to the appointment and discharge of the members of the Supervisory Board and the responsible editor. ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u234	Thank you very much. Hello! The proposal for a law amending the Law on the Slovenian Press Agency in the framework of the public service imposes the publication of news in the English language on voluntary work and NGOs. The Slovenian Press Agency Act in Article 4 lays down the activities of the Agency. Thus, the STA carries out a public service of daily creation, preparation, archiving and transmission of news summaries containing key, most essential information on the event, on the events in the Republic of Slovenia and the world in Slovenian with the following areas: politics, economy, culture, education, health, chronicle, sport, events from the world, science, volunteer work and NGOs, entertainment and other content. In the second paragraph of the same Article, the Act provides that the STA shall perform the public service of daily production, preparation, archiving and transmission of news summaries containing key and most relevant information on the event in the Republic of Slovenia in English; and shall list several areas in which it reports in English and shall summarise the others with the term “other content”. The proposed party Levica, List of Marjan Šarc, Alenka Bratušek and Social Democrats have introduced a legislative procedure to supplement the law, in which translations of voluntary work reports and NGOs are also explicitly highlighted. In the Slovenian Democratic Party, we believe that the existing law already provides for the possibility of translating all the areas reported by the STA, so there is no need to supplement the existing law. On the contrary, we believe that there are unnecessary additions and thus increased red tape. In Slovenia, there are tens of thousands of regulations, laws, regulations and regulations that do not always increase the safety and comfort of citizens and citizens, but sometimes make life more difficult because of their lack of transparency. The coalition agreement is based on, and we can see that the Government of the Republic of Slovenia is trying to implement its commitment to debirocratisation, which we support; and in this spirit, we believe that it is more necessary to direct power to solutions and legal bases that create new opportunities for citizens and citizens to live better, so we do not support the proposal of the proposer. Thank you very much.

Both areas have several positive effects on society and cannot be denied. The draft law was tabled by the Government. With regard to this bill, the Group of 12 Members and Members of the First-signed Matjaž Han requested that the National Assembly hold a general debate. ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u252	Dear Presidents, honourable Members and other participants in today's meeting. We have prepared a proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Housing Law, which we are considering today, at the Ministry of Environment and Space, in response to the difficult access to suitable apartments that we are facing in Slovenia. With the proposed novelty, we want to stop the trend of the collapse of the public rental fund and create sustainable conditions for the operation of housing funds. The novelty contains only urgent changes to the law, which will in a relatively short time have positive effects and will not require additional administrative burdens. We are aware that further steps will have to be taken towards the systematic revision of housing legislation to the proposed solutions. In-depth coordination with all stakeholders in housing policy will be needed as a broad consensus is necessary for the successful implementation of the changes. However, the situation in the area of the provision of public rentals requires some immediate action. The municipalities and housing funds with too low rent can no longer provide simple and not widespread reproduction of the housing fund. As a result, housing waiting periods are increasing and the number of non-profit housing beneficiaries who cannot do so is increasing. The main change is the harmonisation of the amount of the non-profit rent. The value of the point for determining the value of the dwelling using which the non-profit rent is calculated was 2,63 euros established on 1 January 2007 and remained unchanged for 14 years, which is a fundamental reason for the collapse of the public rental fund. In order to reflect the real value of the apartment, we coordinate its value with the novel and ensure that its value is revalued for the future. We propose that the value of the point will gradually increase over a period of three years from the current 2.63 euros to 3.5 euros. This is foreseen every year to align the value of the point with the annual increase in prices of living needs. We are also introducing changes to the rental subsidy system to ensure that the harmonisation of the amount of non-profit rent will not affect socially disadvantaged renters of non-profit apartments. The maximum share of the rent covered by the subsidy shall be raised, a special relief for single-member households and a correction for the calculation of the non-profit rent for disabled persons who are permanently linked to the use of a wheelchair. In order to encourage investment in the construction or acquisition of new public rental apartments, we propose a higher allowable borrowing of housing funds; we allow both state and municipal funds to borrow up to 50% of the intended assets. At the same time, we want to give the State Housing Fund a preliminary right to sell habitable municipal land for multi-housing construction. In particular, I would like to point out the establishment of a public rental service aimed at the activation of an existing but unoccupied housing fund. She'll be an intermediary, a manager, and a tenant. It will relieve owners of all transactions and risks related to the provision of housing and will provide them with regular income in the long term. The acquired apartments will then be handed over to the beneficiaries for rent after a non-profit rent. We are also introducing some key changes in the area of multi-housing buildings management. Mandatory separate fiduciary accounts will be introduced for the reserve fund of each multi-family building, which will contribute to safer and more transparent management of reserve funds. Solutions are also envisaged that will increase the control of the senior owners of the operation of the administrator and in the event of violations, including the action of the house inspection, thereby ensuring, in the first place, the supervision of the manager's conduct and the availability of funds from the reserve fund, which are property of the key owners. With the legal protection of the voting owners, we also increase the necessary consensus to carry out business in multi-housing buildings. This will facilitate the way to maintenance and reconstruction works, energy renovations and the removal of architectural barriers. Multi-inhabitation buildings, which are mostly over 30 years of age, certainly need these interventions in order to provide their inhabitants with an adequate standard of living, but it has been difficult to obtain sufficient consent due to fragmented ownership so far. As you can see, this is a law that truly refers to the most pressing areas of housing policy in Slovenia, so the Ministry believes it should be adopted and implemented as quickly as possible. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u253	Thank you very much. We are moving on to a presentation on the views of the groups of Members. The position of the Group of Ambassadors of Marjan Šarca will be presented by Aljaž Kovacič. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2021-03-03-SDZ8-Izredna-63.ana.u254	Thank you for your word, chairman. Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues! The solutions in the proposed Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Housing Law are similar in their content to the solutions in the proposed amendment to the Housing Law, which was prepared in the government of Marjana Šarc. This proposed novel also addresses the area of housing policy, which can be gravely divided into three interconnected clusters: the relationship between the top tenants of multi-entity buildings, the relationship between the top tenants and the managers of multi-entity buildings, and the strengthening of the public tenant housing fund. The proposed amendment reduces the percentage of the necessary consents of the floor owners in a separate multi-household building to improve or intervene in the joint parts of the building, which are classified by the Construction Act as one of the actions for which a building permit is to be obtained. The proposed solution is based on the implementation of the law in daily practice, as part of which it has been found that a legal percentage of the consent of the floor owners for interventions in the joint parts of the building is often not available. With the implementation of the proposed solution, investments will be made in each multi-housing building to raise the quality of the stay of the top-level owner or user of the apartments and investments that follow the energy efficiency and earthquake safety guidelines. They will also affect the market value of the individual housing unit. The implementation of such investments may, however, jeopardise the condition that the consent of all senior owners is also necessary for the rental and repayment of the loan at the burden of the reserve fund. We believe in the Marjan Sarca List that it would be wise to reduce the required scope of the necessary consensus, as proposed for the execution of interventions and investments for which a building permit is to be obtained. This measure would enable individual housing communities to obtain financial resources for the realisation of those investments that exceed the financial capacity of the reserve funds of each housing community. The proposed novel addresses the shortcomings of the current Housing Law, which are reflected in the relationship between the high-level owners and the managers of multi-housing buildings. With the introduction of separate fiduciary accounts for each apartment building where the funds of the reserve funds are collected, it is explicitly stated that the manager can only have the resources of the reserve fund under the authority of the authorised floor owner of each multi-entity building. The extension of the powers of the competent housing inspectors strengthens the legal position and interest of the senior owners and restricts or completely prevents the possibility of the misuse or disposal of the funds of the reserve fund of the individual housing community by the contract managers of the apartment building. Changes in the protection of the interests of high-level owners are necessary and reflect extreme cases of everyday practice, which resulted in financial damage to high-level owners as payers to housing community reserve funds. The proposed Housing Law, which was drafted in the government of Marjana Sarca, aims to strengthen the public housing fund and implement the Resolution on the National Housing Programme 2015-2025. To this end, measures have been taken to encourage the owners of uninhabited dwellings to put them on the real estate market and measures to establish an appropriate financial basis for the construction of public rental apartments at national and local level. The proposed novel follows the same objectives and measures to establish an appropriate financial basis for the construction of public rental apartments at national and local level vary slightly. The departure from the proposed measures of the new Housing Law from the time of the government of Marjan Šarc is the maintenance of the Institute of Non-profit Rent. Instead of introducing the cost rent that was recognised as a systemic solution at the time of the proposal of the Housing Law, the proposal of the novel retains a non-profit rent and increases the value points for calculating this rent. As a result, non-profit rents will rise and the amount of subsidies for socially disadvantaged people will also rise. In the previous term, the proposed amendment to the Housing Law assessed that maintaining a non-profit rent constituted a non-systemic solution that did not impose mandatory consideration of the value of land which varied strongly in the Republic of Slovenia. The proposal of the novel so provides that the location factor will be taken into account when calculating the rent subsidy. Next. The proposed novel retains the possibility of more indebtedness of the Housing Fund of the Republic of Slovenia and municipal housing funds. The text does not show the possibility of the Housing Fund of the Republic of Slovenia providing guarantees for loans from individuals, young families who first solve the housing issue and the form of employment or monthly income does not allow them to do so. Among the measures to encourage the owners of uninhabited dwellings to place them on the real estate market, the proposal of the novel retains the establishment of a public rental service which would manage, maintain and broadcast them on behalf of and on behalf of the owners of the apartment. Among the measures to increase the number of new buildings for public rentals, the proposed amendment does not provide for the establishment of housing cooperatives. The latter are the main incentives of non-profit housing abroad. They were also very widespread in Slovenia and the former Yugoslavia in the 1960s and 1970s. This measure also misses the professional public in the light of the activities already carried out in the framework of the project on the construction of cooperative dwellings in Rakova Jelša. We also believe in the List of Marjan Šarc that such a measure would further contribute to the implementation of the Resolution on the National Housing Programme 2015-2025. In the List of Marjan Šarca, we welcome the efforts and concrete measures aimed at solving the housing problem. We are in this kind of debate, and we will always act as a constructive interlocutor. We stress, however, that adequate housing policy represents only a stone in the mosaic of a broader consensus between representatives of all social groups – young, elderly, socially endangered. The interests of social groups are very intertwined. For young and socially disadvantaged people with adequate housing, we co-create conditions for the creation of independent lives and allow them to join active representatives of society, which consequently affects the strengthening of intergenerational relations, which form the basis for the creation of intergenerational solidarity. This also creates the conditions for quality and active ageing that strengthens the ability to live decently, and especially enables the elderly to strengthen their position as full members of our society. Thank you.
